ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-02-sejm-69-1.u18	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! During the 68th meeting of the Sejm the first reading of the government draft law on the amendment of the Act on personal income tax was held. The purpose of amending this law is to introduce further simplifications in the settlement of tax returns by taxpayers of personal income tax. The government proposes to introduce a new form of tax settlement in addition to the existing tax statements by the taxpayer, i.e. in paper or electronic form through the e-Report system. [[Game in the O.R., bell ringing.]] tax return by the National Tax Administration, without the need for the taxpayer to submit an application. The National Tax Administration's tax return will be based on information from personal income tax payers, data provided by the taxpayer in the tax return for the previous year and data contained in the register of its own head of the National Tax Administration and other state registers, e.g. ZUS. In practice, this means that close to 25% clearing taxpayers who are individually and who do not benefit from any reductions or deductions will not have to do anything to settle their personal income tax for the previous year. This is a revolutionary qualitative change introduced by the government in the area of PIT settlement. The Public Finance Commission considered this draft law at its meeting on 13 September this year. The Commission has examined the various amendments in all the articles of the Act. Two amendments were tabled during the debate, which were accepted by all members of the committee after discussion and evaluation by the Legislative Bureau. On behalf of the Committee on Public Finance, I recommend the High House to adopt this bill without amendment. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-02-sejm-69-1.u20	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present the position of the Law and Justice Club vis-à-vis the government's draft law amending the Personal Income Tax Act after its second reading, which took place on 13 September in the Public Finance Commission. The legal solutions adopted in the draft for the submission of tax returns on personal income tax are not controversial and accepted by all clubs. Let me remind you: the government in the project is proposing that tax returns be drawn up by the National Tax Administration. When making a statement, the IRS will use already held data about the taxpayer. The taxpayer will have an insight into such a prepared statement, and he will be able to correct it. In the end, the taxpayer will decide whether he will benefit from such testimony or whether he will do so himself from the beginning. It should be pointed out that the taxpayer is protected by the provisions of this law against the negative consequences of the misconception of the tax return by the KAS. During the second reading in the Committee on Public Finance, Members welcomed the technical and editorial amendments which improve the transparency of the rules and do not change the rules in substance. In addition, they welcomed two substantive amendments. The first of these amendments sets out the system for the transmission of data by the ZUS to the central tax register. In this amendment the obligation to transmit data was waived on the CSO [[Game in the O.R., bell ringing.]] on the taxable person, because the same data of the Social Security Office is required to report to the heads of the tax office and these data will be used in the preparation of the statement. The second amendment, which also concerns data provided by the Social Security Office, was also positive. This amendment regulates that tax returns drawn up in 2020 may contain a full entry for the taxpayer, and therefore the Social Security Office is obliged to transfer to the central data register also the data of the taxpayer for December 2018. The amended amendment will make the tax return prepared in 2020 by the tax authority for 2019 covered all contributions paid by the taxpayer in that calendar year. The Law and Justice Club is in favour of these amendments. At the same time, I would like to point out that during the proceedings of the finance committee a discussion was held on the change of deadline, the extension of the deadline for submitting tax returns for the tax year. The government proposes that all taxpayers send information to the head of the tax office by the end of January of the following tax year. This is dictated by the fact that the government is primarily aimed at making refunds as soon as possible for taxable persons overpaid tax. Finally, this request was not supported, as the extension of the deadline for submission of data, information by the taxpayer would also result in a delay in the refund of the excess tax, and, as I said earlier, the purpose of the changes proposed by the government is to return the excess tax to the taxpayers as soon as possible. Thank you very much for your attention. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-02-sejm-69-1.u21	Thank you, Member. I invite Mrs Zofia Czernow to the floor to present her speech on behalf of the Civic Platform. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-02-sejm-69-1.u22	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform has the honour to present to the High House a position on the government draft law amending the Act on personal income tax and certain other laws, print Nos 2809 and 2843. The draft amendment to the Personal Income Tax Act and some other laws introduces further simplification of PIT tax settlements. The comprehensive IT system of the tax administration was launched by the PO-PSL government, introducing the possibility of submitting tax returns via the Internet using the free e-Tests application. The use of the declaration made available on the website of the Ministry of Finance did not entail the need for qualified electronic signature. Only data from the accounts of the previous year were needed for verification. The next step was the introduction in 2015 of a pre-completed PIT settlement, which could have been accepted by the taxpayer in the absence of reliefs and other comments. The Ministry of Finance continued to develop this solution, which allows further facilitation for taxpayers and which are included in the project under investigation. According to the draft law, the National Tax Administration will prepare completed annual tax returns for all taxpayers, which will be made available from 15 February of the year following the tax year until 30 April in an electronic version in the application available on the tax portal. The National Tax Administration will be based on information from payers, data provided by the taxpayer in a statement for the previous year, e.g. relief for children, no KRS public benefit organization, which last year gave 1% the tax, and the data contained in the records of the head of KAS concerning the amount of advances paid on income tax and other registers, in particular the Social Security Office. In the 2018 accounts, statements will be available to taxable persons who are not engaged in business activities and who submit a tax return on the form PIT-37, and to taxable persons who obtain income from cash capital, making a tax return on the form PIT-38. In the following year, tax returns will also be available to taxpayers of natural persons engaged in non-agricultural activities. It should be stressed that taxpayers whose contact details are in the register of cash registers, e.g. telephone number, e-mail address, etc., will receive a message about your tax return. The taxpayer will choose to proceed. It can verify and accept the settlement unchanged. In the case of PIT-37 and PIT-38 statements, no action may be taken. They will then be automatically accepted at the end of the deadline for the submission of annual statements. It can modify and supplement data, e.g. change the organisation of public benefit and other data that the National Tax Administration does not have and then accept. It can, of course, reject the settlement and settle itself. The introduction of the submission will further simplify the tax system for individuals' income tax payers by removing the obligation to produce PIT tax returns on their own. This will also avoid errors and other stress that often accompany taxpayers in this whole PIT settlement procedure. For taxable persons making PITs by electronic communication there will also be a reduction, which is very important, from 3 months to 45 days of reimbursement of the excess payment resulting from the submission of the statement. Reducing the costs of tax collection and saving paper and protecting forests, which are closely linked to this, will have an important effect on simplifications. The Civic Platform Parliamentary Club welcomes the continued work undertaken during our governments, the PO-PSL governments, on the simplification of taxpayers' accounts. We will vote in favour of the bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-02-sejm-69-1.u24	Thank you very much. Of course, I'm joining you in welcoming the high school students. This is the greatest time of my life. Unfortunately, the studies are a little different. We're waiting for you down here because it's time to rejuvenate a little. It's company. That includes me. On the other hand, back to rejuvenation, we are starting here a discussion on simplification, right? Because the aim of these changes is to simplify, make it easier to account for taxes. This is, of course, the right direction, which is the European direction. We are even a little late here, in some countries this process is far more advanced. I understand that this is not only a matter of willingness, but also capacity, mainly IT systems, and these information systems in our country are unfortunately neither integrated nor working well enough. I would very much like such changes to be parallel to changes and powerful investments in information systems, but to create an e-state whose functioning would make life easier for all of us and would not create such absurdities as I find in my professional work. When e.g. PESEL makes a request for a PESEL number when it is required by a person living abroad, who is also a Polish citizen, but who does not have an important Polish passport, the office has to conduct proceedings at the voivodeship for confirmation of citizenship, even though it has the possibility to look at it on the computer, to trace this process and should know. Something's not right about this, but this bill has to go in the right direction. We will therefore, in principle, support it, of course, but this does not mean that the project is flawless. My previous speaker, or rather your rapporteur, spoke about these comments we have been talking about. We want to make it easier for the taxpayer, but let's remember that taxpayers, and in this case It is the payers who are also entrepreneurs. We cannot proceed in such a way as to choke entrepreneurs, and undoubtedly shorten the deadline for the submission of annual tax calculations from 2 months, as has been the case so far, to 1 month causes them huge problems. Really, the beginning of the year is the time when all reports are being prepared and at this moment the additional duty is added in January... January is not a normal month. This is the month in which we have much less working days, as the beginning of January de facto is excluded from normal work. Large entrepreneurs have accounting services, tax services, but small entrepreneurs will have a very complicated situation, it will be difficult in such a short time to prepare all this information, also documentation, and we know that this documentation is crucial because the reliability of this information depends on the next stage of settlement and reimbursement, which we are talking about, possible overpayments. From that sort of thing. Well, that depends. So that we are constantly repeating the proposal here, we are hoping that, in the further process, perhaps at the stage of the Senate, we will be able to find a compromise. The proposal for this compromise was presented. Since there can no longer be a 2-month period because we want to shorten this process, this burden should be spread equally between the National Tax Administration and entrepreneurs. Therefore, it was proposed that this should not be the end of January, but only be the middle of February. I think it is still worthwhile for us to discuss this, sit down with specialists and think about whether this is possible. In our view, it is possible for the National Tax Administration to take appropriate action during this period, which will remain. Let us remember that this is not the best solution if we introduce simplifications for the citizen and this is happening at someone else's expense. This should be done in such a way that our administration is to work more and more, to be more equipped, to have more and better tools and thus to be faster, more efficient and to make life easier not only for us taxpayers, but also for payers, entrepreneurs. ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-02-sejm-69-1.u28	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Club of the Polish People's Party - Union of European Democrats, I have the honour to present my position on the report of the committee on the government draft law amending the Act on income tax on individuals and certain other laws, prints No. 2809 and 2843. The bill proposes to continue the electronicisation of annual tax accounts in personal income tax, while leaving the current form of paper and electronic accounting for itself by the taxpayer. According to the project, the tax services of KAS will prepare an annual tax return, which may be accepted or verified by the taxpayer. At present, this form of settlement works for pensioners and pensioners with the possibility to provide information on the disposal of 1% a tax on the organisation of public benefit. The current project will cover clearing taxpayers individually, together with spouses and single parents already in the 2018 accounts. On the other hand, economic operators will be able to use this form for the 2019 accounts. The annual tax return prepared by KAS will be made available to taxpayers in electronic form, in an application available on the tax portal from 15 February each year after the end of the tax year. The taxpayer will have access to this statement after authentication with a free trusted profile or authorization data. The testimony prepared by the KAS will be available to the taxpayer between 15 February and 30 April: verify and accept without change, improve as a result of the verification carried out, e.g. the amount of revenue costs, to be added to the data that KAS does not have, e.g. tax relief, or reject and settle in other ways, e.g. in the form of a previous one. On the other hand, if the taxpayer fails to act, the statement prepared by the tax authorities will be deemed acceptable. The work of the Committee on Public Finance discussed the dates of the submission of information and settlements. Finally, the version proposed by the government remains. Thus, the deadlines for employers and pension bodies to submit declarations at the end of February will be reduced by the end of January using only electronic form. In addition, the committee accepted a number of legislative amendments tabled by the Legislative Office and adopted two substantive amendments. The amendments adopted concern the modification and clarification of information submitted by the Social Insurance Institution. In conclusion, it should be stressed that the submitted project is a good, modern solution using the possibilities of electronic communication and the Polish People's Party Club - the European Union of Democrats will vote in favour of it.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-02-sejm-69-1.u30	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I have the honour to present on behalf of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs a report on the Government's draft law on public documents, contained in print No 2153. Mr Marshal, in accordance with the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, previously referred this draft law to first reading in the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs. The Commission considered the same draft at its meetings on 7 and 27 February and 11 and 18 September and asked the High Sejm to adopt the attached draft law. In order to approximate the solutions contained in the report relating to this project, I would like to draw attention to a few key ones, all the more so because it is a very important project, it can be said in simplification that the so-called. a system for the production of public documents. I would like to remind you that the main aim of this project is to create an effective system for the security of public documents, and practice shows that even the best secured documents without a sealed system are not and cannot be sufficiently protected from counterfeiting, from being used to commit crimes. The need for the regulations provided for in the draft law on public documents is primarily demonstrated by the practice of fraud and other illegal activities carried out by means of falsified documents. On average in Poland, statistically, among the more than one million criminal offences committed annually about 6% are crimes against the credibility of documents, and in this group of economic crimes of this type represent as much as 40%. So we have an urgent need to address these phenomena in this context. And that is the answer to the government presentation that we have worked here during the meetings of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs, which I mentioned. The most desirable documents by criminals are documents which forgery creates an opportunity to extort large amounts of money from the state or create even a new identity of a person, and the causes of such a phenomenon are very many. This is supported primarily by technological progress, greater access to modern devices that help to forge documents, as well as the development of new forms of business, migration movements, but also the lack of systemic solutions related to document security, including so-called. distributed production of public documents, a low level of protection against counterfeiting of certain of these documents, and a lack of unified models of public documents. The government project discussed and worked at the meetings of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs in a way, I think, comprehensively introduces the necessary solutions for the security of public documents, starting with the design of the document sheet, through its storage, production, verification of authenticity by public officials. Here it is much stronger and will be the establishment of the Polish Securities Printing Works as the entity to which the State orders the implementation of this law in particular. The draft law sets out general standards for the production of public document blanks. These sheets of documents most important for security reasons will just be produced by the previously mentioned single-member company of the Treasury, the subject of which is the production of sheets of documents and highly secured prints. The public documents of category 2 and 3 will be produced by entities fulfilling the requirements laid down in the proposed law and implementing rules issued under that law. There is also a solution contained in this project, which boils down to the model of the online register of all public documents, and this is and will be a source of knowledge for public officials, citizens and officials so that it can be verified correctly whether the document with which they are in contact is authentic or should raise some doubts. And it is also an opportunity to equalize this, you can say in quotes, fight against counterfeiters. The Act also responds to the phenomenon of creating so-called. Collector's documents. This involves introducing documents that resemble original documents in their appearance to the general public. Where such documents are produced, offered and disposed of, appropriate criminal provisions are provided for in that law. The Minister of Administration will be responsible for the process of shaping the security policy of public documents in Poland, sorry, Minister of Home Affairs and Administration. In this department, public documents will be properly designed against counterfeiting. There will be an analysis of these frauds, which will also allow for a very rapid response when it comes to breaking down, and I apologize, which will allow for a rapid response in terms of possible breach by counterfeiters of the security system in documents. The Resort together with institutions such as the Internal Security Agency will also be responsible for the control of bodies producing public documents. To sum up, ladies and gentlemen, I would like to stress that the bill goes against the long-standing demands of environments that deal with the security of documents. Proper protection against counterfeiting, reliable methods of verification of their authenticity are important from the point of view of every citizen. They are also important for the certainty of legal trading, for the certainty of economic and financial trading. This law adopted here without a political dispute, which I would like to stress, provides a legal basis for building a public document security system. The amendments that were introduced by the Members at the committee's meeting were, as I would like to stress, some drafting and legislative changes. We have also waited for European case law for a time. It concerned an Austrian case where a similar solution was introduced to the proposed law. As a result, as I would like to stress, I strongly recommend that the High House continue its work and adopt this government bill on public documents. On behalf of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs, I would like to thank all the ladies and gentlemen for working together on a good cause. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-02-sejm-69-1.u31	Thank you for presenting the report. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. I ask Mrs Marta Kubiak to speak on behalf of the Law and Justice Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-02-sejm-69-1.u32	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I present to the Sejm of the Republic of Poland the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice in relation to the draft law on public documents, print No 2153. The Public Documents Act sets itself the main objective of creating such safeguards for public documents that would be effective enough to ensure the greatest possible security for the state and its citizens. The lack of a sealed system leads to a number of offences related to the counterfeiting of documents, even the most sensitive ones. Accession to the Schengen area has strengthened the internal security system of the country, but has led to the birth of unknown cross-border crime. The main problem with the current state of affairs is the dispersion of documents in nearly 400 legal acts. It is extremely important to improve the security of public documents that a number of procedures be organised in this area of public activity in order to guarantee the highest standards of protection. In order to achieve maximum benefits, it is necessary to properly establish the order of public documents, to establish design procedures, to establish a list of documents and to lay down rules for their storage. The essence of the project is the establishment of a body responsible for shaping the security policy of public documents. Designed solutions are important for the Republic of Poland and for every citizen. They provide certainty and guarantee the security of the legal area of the country. The proposed project mainly regulates the concept of a public document. The public document within the meaning of the project is only a document secured. The project classified public documents for their validity. The first category concerns the most important documents from the point of view of state security. These are among others. IDs, passports and registrations. Legal and notarial documents have been classified into the same category as a result of recognition of their fundamental importance for State security. The second category will include public documents relevant for reasons of state security, economic and legal security. The third category was covered by documents affecting the safety of economic and legal trade. For each of these categories a minimum protection against counterfeiting has been provided. The project sets out general standards for the production and production of public documents ensuring the safety of production. The most important models for the security of documents, which belong to the first group, will be carried out by a single-member company of the State Treasury, which will guarantee the highest level of security. The granting of identification documents to a company supervised by the State, which is characteristic of most European Union countries, will guarantee the security of the personal data of citizens for whom identification documents are issued. On the other hand, the company's production of secured documents used to carry out various types of transactions will guarantee security in this area as well. The project also lays down standards for the production and production of public documents belonging to the second and third categories. Detailed requirements for the security of production of category 2 and third public document blanks will be laid down by the Minister responsible for Home Affairs after consulting the Head of the Internal Security Agency. The proposed project also presents a security control and guarantee tool, which is the Public Document Register. This relates to the definition of the issuer's obligations as regards the transmission of information on the new model of the document and possible means of verifying its authenticity. Data on public documents and cases of counterfeiting will be collected in the register. Every citizen through such a register available on-line will be able to use the models of the documents in force, together with their description, as well as a description of the possibility of verifying their authenticity. The introduction of such a high level of security for public documents and the reliable control of the authenticity of these documents by public officials will produce positive results and will create a comprehensive tool for the implementation of the State's obligation to protect public documents. On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I recommend a bill to be adopted. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-02-sejm-69-1.u46	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report to the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs on the revision of the Act on State Medical Rescue. The first reading of this project took place at a committee meeting, so maybe it is worth to say a little in addition to the work report itself. on the reasons why we need to make this change today. The Act aims to correct a certain error, which was included in the Act amending the Act on State Medical Rescue of 10 May 2018. That law created a system of units, created a register in which all rescue units that cooperate with the State Medical Rescue should be entered. Such a register shall be kept by voivodships and entities that deal with water rescue, mountain rescue, e.g. Mining rescue, they have to sign in to that register. This type of action is indeed ongoing. Today, WOPR or TOPR and other entities actually enter this register kept by the Ministry of Health. On the other hand, the Act also included two provisions that could have caused very serious problems, if not disaster, in the area of water rescue and in the mountains. Well, there are provisions there, Art. 15 and Article 15 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers pursuant to Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council and repealing Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 (OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 1). 16 - they said that the relevant entities that are involved in mountain rescue and water rescue must... that the authorisation to carry out rescue activities issued by the Ministry of Interior and Administration expires on 31 December 2018. The inclusion of this provision in the Act was not intended and could lead to a situation in which after 1 January 2019 there will simply be no entities in Poland that could be involved in mountain rescue and water rescue. I would like to remind you that when the Act on Water Rescue and Mountain Rescue entered into force - it was the 2011 Act - the entities that carried out water rescue and mountain rescue prior to the entry into force of the Act had 24 months to obtain the permission of the Minister of Interior and Administration to carry out such activities. Getting that consent was really time-consuming. In the case of large WOPRs, these were often applications, which counted at 4,6 thousand. the parties, because these applications contained not only information on the activity itself but also information on the privileges of water rescuers. It was therefore very often the case that this procedure was simply very long. At present, in my opinion and in the opinion of the committee, there is no possibility that this condition, which is provided for by the Act of 10 May 2018, means that it is about re-applying to the Minister for consent and obtaining these consents... There's no real chance of getting that kind of consent again. Moreover, it seems pointless since these entities are still carrying on such activities. Let me remind you that there are only 120 WOPRs in Poland. These are, of course, individuals of different sizes, while imposing an additional obligation on them to obtain consent is simply not intended. Therefore, the Commission's initiative aims to amend these provisions relating to the granting of consent and to bring about a situation in which individuals who obtain entry into the voivodship register of cooperating entities can continue to carry out rescue activities. Here we are giving up this cezure so far designated by the law, with the need to obtain a new entry which would allow the operation after 1 January 2019. High Izbo! This law is, as I mentioned, a law that corrects a mistake, which is why it is important for it to enter into force as soon as possible, so that the situation that is taking place simply corrects it. It is therefore justified to enter into force as soon as possible. Here we propose that we move away from this 14-day vacathio legis, so that the legal situation of those individuals who are involved in mountain and water rescue is simply clear as soon as possible so that they are not in a situation of certain ignorance about what to do, whether or not to try to re-submit these applications. We believe that in this case, the withdrawal from this 14-day vacathio legis is most appropriate, and this is the opinion shared by the committee, such a discussion took place in the committee. In the committee we also introduced some editorial amendments to the original bill, while the sense is the same, i.e. this requirement for re-approval is replaced by this requirement for entry in the register of cooperating entities, to those registers which are kept by voivodships. This will lead to a situation where this entry procedure will be much less complicated. In this respect, we hope that by the end of the year these units will be able to enter and will be able to lead to such a situation that, after 1 January 2019, all entities that have been involved in mountain or water rescue will still be able to carry out these activities. On the committee, all the clubs supported this bill. Thank you so much for that. I would also like to thank you for your involvement on the part of the Legislative Office, because this is the case for committee projects - without the involvement of the Legislative Office of the Sejm, such projects would simply not have been created. In this case, We acted very quickly. We noticed this mistake a few weeks ago, and actually today we have a second reading. We hope that this law will be passed at this meeting. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The task of this fund will be to finance the construction, reconstruction and renovation of district and municipal roads, as well as the construction of bridges located in local roads. Voivodeships will also evaluate applications for funding. On the basis of the evaluation carried out, a list of tasks recommended for funding from the Fund will be drawn up. These letters will be subject to verification by the Minister of Infrastructure in financial terms. The final list of tasks to be supported will be approved by the President of the Council of Ministers. This right stems from the fact that the President of the Council of Ministers is responsible not only for government policy but also for the entire country. The grant will be granted to qualified applicants on the basis of a contract concluded between the voivodship and the relevant local government unit. Euros. At present, this revenue threshold entitles 15% of the rate to be applied. We want to reduce this rate to 9% in order to activate entrepreneurs, say, small and medium-sized, who are about 400,000. operators currently registered and entitled to apply this rate. In addition, we provide incentives to leave capital for development in companies. We're introducing the so-called. a hypothetical deduction of interest on retained earnings and on company subsidies. This hypothetical deduction is that we want in some way to align the tax effects of the financing that a taxpayer can obtain outside, i.e. debt financing, with its own funding. We predict that the deduction from this hypothetical interest rate on equity will amount to 2,5%. On a yearly basis, with the upper limit, the top limit will be 250,000. zł. We are introducing amendments relating to a 5-year period for the exemption from the sale of real estate. This approach of the tax authorities leads to the situation that inheritance of property after the spouse, parents, grandparents is treated as a re-purchase of real estate or rights to real estate, which in fact previously constituted the property of the heir, in particular. in the case of the property community of the spouses. It is quite important here to stress that we have a much greater nuisance of the current solution for citizens who are covered by such a right, in relation to the benefits which, on the other hand, flow from this provision to the state budget. ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-02-sejm-69-1.u192	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! High Izbo! I will refer to questions that were substantive in nature. The first question was about divorce and separation. The subsequent abolition of this community as a result of divorce or separation does not mean that the term ceases to count. So... [[Paulin Hennig-Kloska: You do not know the caselaw in this case.]] ...our project provides that both divorces and separations will be counted from the moment of acquisition or construction within the statutory community. Another issue was the reduction of CIT to 9%. The question referred to the limit of 2 million - whether this is the result of a mess or an oversight. This is not the result of a mess or omission, it is our conscious decision that we want this 9 percent very low rate, really rare in developed countries, to apply only and exclusively to revenues that do not exceed the equivalent of 1200 thousand. Euros. To clarify this a little bit, I will say that this limit in our project will be calculated on the net amount, i.e. it will not include this amount of VAT. The third question was whether it would be better to re-establish in the new Tax Order a material legal term. This is a matter... that's what the new Tax Order is planning to do. I'm working on a new Tax Order, too, and that's the solution we're going to put in. On the other hand, we felt that it was better not to wait for the new Tax Order, that it was better just to introduce this solution, which allows spouses and single parents to benefit from common accounting as soon as possible. This solution for the restoration of the material legal term will be applicable to other issues where such a restoration would be desirable, and here it seems to us that for social reasons, however, it would be worth speeding up and hence this presentation which I have discussed here today. Another issue is the issue of passenger cars. I understand that you're assuming that at the moment it is so that the entrepreneur can deduct 100% the costs resulting from the use of the car, while we introduce as if a limitation, because we say: it can only deduct 75%. It's not like that. In practice, the situation is now in such a way that the trader can deduct as much as is related to income. So if he uses the car in its entirety to conduct business and does not use it for private purposes, then he can actually deduct 100% costs. However, if it were to come, say, tax control and really wanted to see if it was using it for business purposes, it might not be. That's why I think this is our solution, which allows us to deduct 75%. costs without documentation, is an orderly and business-friendly solution. On the other hand, those entrepreneurs who, as you said, use the car almost 24 hours a day in such a way that they sleep in it that they carry out this activity and use it as an office will indeed be able to deduct 100% expenditure. I apologize for my slip on small and medium-sized entrepreneurs - I should have said: micro-entrepreneurs, and this is really about small taxpayers. That's the truth. That's what I should have said. This concerns small taxpayers, not medium-sized or small entrepreneurs within the meaning of Business Law. There was a question about the reporter's release. Our estimates indicate that this problem concerns about 15% taxpayers. We'll look into it and consider what we can do about it. We've been thinking about that, and we haven't yet prepared a solution to this problem about, let's say, 15%. - I don't know if these estimates are correct, so I'm saying about. Let's see if there's actually a simple solution here. The next question is how much we're going to pull out of entrepreneurs' pockets because we're going to introduce, as I understand it, these requirements for... this limit of 150,000. or that deduction which is related to the limitation - up to 75% expenditure. It's not like we're pulling something out of entrepreneurs' pockets. We're raising the limit on cars that have been frozen for a very long time. I don't know, since I started taxing, I think that limit was about 80,000, 20,000. The euro was equivalent to about 80,000. zł. Therefore, it seems to me that raising this limit to 150 thousand. evil is a reality of the situation, but I also think that most micro-entrepreneurs do not buy such expensive cars in practice. I think it's a $150,000 limit. is such a limit, which is easy to rationally justify, because it actually allows for the purchase of a quite decent car and the cost of this expenditure, which is, as you rightly claim, in many cases this basic component of the micro-company. As for the Modern Project, I appreciate your work on this project and I appreciate your leaning on this problem. In my speech, I wanted to say that our project contains the solutions you are proposing and goes on, i.e. proposes solutions that are a little further along the lines of the project, giving greater cost deductions than those you proposed in your project. That's why I think our project is better because of it. So in the project that we submit in print no.2854, we propose that in the event of the sale acquired by inheritance of real estate, this 5-year period should be counted from the end of the calendar year in which the estate was acquired or built by the heirs. In the event of disposal after the cessation of the matrimonial property communiqué acquired into the joint assets or built during the duration of the matrimonial property communiqué, this period of five years shall also be calculated from the end of the calendar year in which they were acquired into the joint property of the spouses or their construction. These are solutions that are similar, the same. [[Paulin Hennig-Kloska: What about children and grandchildren?]] Children and grandchildren, if they inherit, will count from the moment... [[Paulin Hennig-Kloska: These are the successors. This isn't a property community.]] Yeah, but I'm talking in the first... I take it we're talking about acquisition by inheritance, aren't we? Thus, the successions, as they acquire through inheritance, take into account the period that affected the heirs. This solution therefore provides for the case that you have now mentioned here. In addition, our printing project 2854 gives the heirs the right to credit the tax costs of those expenses which have been incurred by the heir for the acquisition or construction, i.e. those expenses which have not yet been disclosed in the sense that he has incurred these expenses. In a normal case, in this state that is now, they would be as if they were lost, wouldn't they? However, we propose, as if to inherit also the cost which the heir suffered, right? Another point that we are proposing is to take into account debts and inheritance burdens as revenue costs. In a normal situation, the heir who inherits receives not only assets but also liabilities, right? We propose that these liabilities be costs when the heir sells. It seems to me that this project is more comprehensive in this respect - maybe let's put it this way - and better solves the problems that arise in the inheritance of real estate. Because I think the Minister realizes that this bow towards the citizens or citizens of our country in this area There is little damage to the state budget, and many Poles at a difficult time in life can really help solve the problem. So if it turns out that your legislation does not regulate it, and I will look at it quite closely, then I will simply have to make appropriate amendments at the stage of the parliamentary committee's work. ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-02-sejm-69-1.u196	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The proposal to amend the laws on income taxes on individuals, corporates and tax administrations provides for certain simplifications in relation to income taxes and, in general, the sealing of the tax system, also implements certain EU directives to some extent. As far as simplification is concerned, or in general the compatibility of the system, these provisions contain comprehensive transfer pricing regulation. This is a regulation that is substantively difficult. We have taken the effort to simplify this regulation and to make it more transparent, above all on legislative issues, by eliminating the bunk references contained in the current legislation. And this regulation that we propose in terms of transfer pricing is really much easier to understand even for an average person, for an average reader than what is in the current regulations. Furthermore, this regulation is linked to such a technological leap, as we propose here an automatic analysis of transfer pricing data, within the framework of, say, reporting documentation, reporting information on transfer pricing applied by taxpayers and on transactions with tax havens. The second important thing is to introduce certain simplifications or friendly solutions for taxpayers. This friendly solution is IP Box, i.e. preferential taxation of income derived from intellectual property. This is a solution to push the Polish economy a little bit towards a more knowledge-based economy, and it is a solution that is internationally accepted by both the OECD and the European Commission, which is being applied in many other countries. In addition, there are a number of such simplifications, facilitations, for example, concerning the acquisition of debt packages, tax write-offs for cooperative banks making payments to the institutional protection system. We will also introduce a very important simplification on collection, in fact the exemption for withholding tax on interest paid on Eurobonds. This is a solution which aims to gather more external financing on the Polish market and facilitate large economic operators to look for this funding outside. In this way we also hope that there will be more credit for smaller companies in Poland. In addition, we are also introducing a completely new regulation on the taxation of income from virtual currencies. As regards the sealing of the tax system, this project provides for the introduction of mandatory reporting of optimization schemes, i.e. a situation in which professionals who advise on how to reduce taxation, often unfortunately using artificial tax structures... We want such plans to be reported to the National Tax Administration. The result of this notification will be an increase in knowledge of planned optimization schemes, as well as the discouragement of these optimizers to apply tax optimization, I would stress the wording: aggressive tax optimization. We will also introduce changes regarding the sealing of the source tax collection system, so-called. royalty tax, dividends and interest. We will introduce a different way of collecting taxes in case of large payments. We want to have more control over what money comes out of Poland. The point is whether they are taxed correctly. We want to have more control over it. We want to be more active in this area. This law provides for e.g. actively using the exchange of tax information by the Polish tax administration. We also introduce sealing changes in the anti-tax avoidance clause, as well as sealing changes in foreign controlled entities, namely CFCs. I will briefly present these solutions, which I mainly discussed here in general. As far as transfer prices are concerned, we are reducing documentation obligations. We extend our reporting deadlines, which means we actually make them real, from 3 to 12 months. We are introducing some facilitations and exemptions for documentation. This law also aims to bring our regulations and documentation requirements into line with the requirements imposed by the OECD. There are some simplifications regarding the rules of settlement, so-called. safe harbors. We comprehensively regulate the rules on the use of market prices in transactions with related parties. We are actually introducing non-existent rules on transfer pricing adjustment. The rules for the correction of invoices were used, but they are not entirely the same. First of all, what is important from the point of view of sealing, we introduce modern reporting principles based on computer systems and macro data analysis, which we will obtain with these reports. We also harmonise certain concepts used in the law. With regard to IP Box, we introduce a 5% rate of taxation of intellectual property income. This is very important because the Polish relief offered by us in this area will be one of the best in Europe. It aims to increase the attractiveness of R &amp; D activities in Poland and to increase employment and stimulate investment in R &amp; D. Such solutions are present in other countries. We introduce a solution, which is a very beneficial solution, because it covers a very broad spectrum of intellectual rights: the right to an invention, the protection right to a utility model, the right resulting from the registration of an industrial design, the topography of a integrated circuit, additional protection rights concerning patents for medicinal products or plant protection products, the right to register a medicinal product and a veterinary treatment product which are authorised to market, and other rights, also relevant copyrights for a computer program. An important feature of this relief, so-called. The relief from IP Box is that we're going to want this revenue to come from the commercialization of intellectual property rights. We want this 5% stake actually contributed to the development of R &amp; D activities. We are also introducing simplification solutions, e.g. concerning the acquisition of sets of claims. We are simplifying some unfavorable provisions on the current rules. As far as cooperative banks are concerned, we are introducing a 50% deduction, a reduction in the tax base for this system of institutional protection. Rules for the taxation of virtual currencies. Since virtual currency trading is an increasingly growing area, we want this exchange as a virtual exchange to remain tax-neutral, while we will tax income from these currencies, i.e. revenue valued as money considered a legal means of payment. An alternative bond taxation system is a solution that will drive large players to look for funding in the eurobonds market. We will abolish taxation at source because we are most often faced with the situation that entities issue Eurobonds to a distributed owner, i.e. for those with whom we do not have contact, often we do not know where their residence is. It is difficult to obtain information that allows the application of tax concessions. We also foresee some abolition arrangements for taxpayers who have already benefited early from this issue of Eurobonds and have applied tax optimization. We are introducing reporting of optimization schemes, as I have already mentioned. These are the solutions implementing Directive 2011/16, which is true to say that we are exchanging information on cross-border schemes, but this is because the European Union has competence in cross-border issues. However, this does not mean that we cannot put in place such reporting in the case of domestic transactions, and such reporting in respect of domestic transactions, we are also introducing, because we want our tax authorities to be at least as well informed about the optimisation schemes as they will be informed to the authorities of the Member States of the European Union for which we will also collect and then forward this information to them in relation to cross-border schemes. The action we are taking also implements the OECD Recommendation to counter the erosion of the tax base. We're making changes to the collection of withholding tax at source. As I said, we intend to introduce greater supervision of large payments, which cause a significant outflow in the tax base from Poland, and we want to control whether the conditions for the benefits provided for by the European directives and the reductions provided for by the double taxation agreements are maintained. As regards the anti-tax avoidance clause, we are partially implementing European Union Directive 2016/1164 called ATAD, namely Anti-Tax Avoidance Directive. We are introducing additional solutions to seal this clause, i.e. sanctions for income, which will be defined as revenue from aggressive tax optimization. This sanction is going to be 10%. that kind of income. In other countries, these sanctions are much higher, e.g. France applies the rate of 80% Depleting height, New Zealand - from 20% to 150% Follow-up, Ireland - 30% backlog, United Kingdom - 60% tax arrears. So I don't think our sanction is going very far. Importantly, we are introducing the possibility of reversing the effects of optimization. We are also introducing an exit tax or exit tax solution. This solution also introduces the ATAD Directive into the Polish tax system. However, in our project, I admit, we extend this solution to individuals. I would like to draw attention to the fact that many European countries also apply such a solution to individuals and that our solution is aimed at fair taxation of the value that was created in Poland. Transfer of assets outside Poland or transfer of tax residence outside Poland causes us to lose this right. Hence we introduce taxation on exit tax, but we also introduce reliefs, e.g. The relief is that if someone decides to return to the country within 5 years, this tax will be refunded. Thank you for listening, and I'm asking you questions. If you have any questions, I would be happy to answer and explain. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-02-sejm-69-1.u220	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Thank you for the questions asked in the discussion. I would like to address your comments and questions. This is a difficult issue, because the deferral in this case unfortunately is a tool that de facto causes the loss of tax power, and we are not able to enforce this obligation after, say, moving the residence abroad. Professor and other Members have asked questions about reporting tax schemes and the secrecy of regulated professions. We have conducted a very extensive pre-consultation. In December 2017, we completed a pre-consultation on the secrecy of regulated professions and took into account the most important probably request from regulated professionals - so that there is no mandatory reporting of the scheme in the case of regulated professions, just to make it possible to choose. The point is that when someone who does a regulated profession asks the customer if he can give up this secret and report it, the customer decides whether he reports himself or whether he allows it to be done by a professional. In both cases, we made a duty for a professional to assist a client to prepare this report. As far as sanctions are concerned, here were questions about whether it even worked, how many decisions there were and how many protective opinions there were. Ladies and gentlemen, the anti-tax avoidance clause operates in such a way that it is applied daily by professionals: auditors, auditors, tax advisers, lawyers, legal advisers, because they realise that such legislation is in the Polish system. If someone is an honest, good professional, they will not expose their client to creating artificial structures, transferring assets to artificially created foundations, to foreign companies that do not carry out any actual activity. That's how the clause works. As regards the question of how many of these decisions were, there was currently no decision based on the general anti-tax avoidance clause. But she, like I said, functions through it... [[Speech Off the Microphone]] There were two protective opinions and two probably negative opinions. I'm not sure, but I can check it out. [[Yes.]] Now for questions about exit tax. We assume that we have the right to tax the increase in the value of assets that occurred in Poland. As for business planning, if someone is planning a business activity in such a way that they want to change their residence, then of course It's possible, it can change its residence. It's okay. On the other hand, if someone plans to do his business in such a way that he wants to change his residence and actually conduct his business in Poland, that is, he wants to benefit from the benefits of the system, which gives him e.g. 2% taxation because it changed residence, but in fact all assets are in Poland, it is not... This is tax optimisation. This is an attempt to show that when I'm in a better system, I want to pay 2%. No. If someone is active in Poland, they pay according to Polish rules. That rule remains. As regards exit tax for the transfer, as Mr Richard Petru mentioned here, the exit in the sense that we are transferring assets, again this is a bit of a misunderstanding. If we make a direct investment abroad, we include shares or shares in the company, i.e. we transfer assets, we can transfer assets, for example. to Ukraine, we create a company there, we receive shares for it, that is okay, because this does not cause the property to be removed from Poland. However, if someone transfers assets in such a way that they physically dismantles the factory that they have in Poland, transfers these assets abroad and creates jobs there, then okay, that is his right, that is his choice. It takes on the risks associated with that market, takes on the risks associated with that legal system. It's okay, it's okay to do that, we just want to have the right to tax any value that has grown on these assets at a time when they were in Poland. There were quite a lot of questions, so why don't we do that so that I don't answer questions right now, during the oral answer, I'll prepare written answers. [[It's about individuals.]] About the naturals. Good. Why are we extending this to individuals? Because, as Mr Petru pointed out here, there is indeed such a phenomenon that individuals form foreign foundations and transfer their assets there. It's an undesirable phenomenon because they don't do it... See, we have introduced the most liberal rules in terms of taxation of succession. There is no tax on succession in the family between loved ones, whether inheritance or donation. And now someone's telling me that he's creating a foreign foundation in Liechtenstein, because this foundation better protects his succession rights. We have introduced a bill on business succession, this law is already in place. From a civil-law point of view, it is secured, from a tax point of view there is no tax on the transfer of assets between family members. So, what kind of business is it that's protected by someone who's moving their assets to a foreign foundation in Liechtenstein? It is intended to counteract this tax, the taxation of assets in the event of a transfer by a natural person abroad. Here I must point out that we are only talking about financial assets, shares, shares in companies, not that you will move out in the sense that you will take a car and family and leave to find happiness in another, cooler system. It's okay, you can do that, only that if someone has a property quite important, because we introduced this exit tax only from the property, which exceeds 4 million PLN... All of us here, in this Chamber, and in general all Poles wish such property at today's prices and according to today's value of this money, that everyone should have so much, but at the moment it is not common, that someone has such property in liquid, financial assets, i.e. shares, shares, bonds. There was a question here concerning e.g. moving implementation times, why we are doing this in 2019 and not waiting for 2020. This is because there has been a situation in recent years that many have moved their wealth. We know that's what happened. They transferred their assets to foundations, which we believe to be foundations aimed at tax planning, to aggressive tax optimization. We want to put an end to this as soon as possible. Now as to why we impose it on individuals and why the European Union only imposes it on companies, it is because the European Union has no competence to impose it on individuals. This is our sovereign decision, every country that imposed this tax. By the way, this is perhaps 10 developed countries which imposed an exit tax. And we're putting it on the basis of our sovereign decision, because we want to protect our tax base from erosion. It's simple. As for the fact that we are doing this in 2019 and not in 2020, this is because we want to have it introduced faster. That's why we do it in 2019. We also know that if we had done this in 2020, those who were actually planning to employ the Luxembourg or Liechtenstein foundation to manage Polish assets would certainly have done so. Because creating such an artificial structure is time-consuming. This requires the formation of companies, going to a notary, transferring assets, doing a number of consulting activities, obtaining valuations. It's not that simple, it's not easy. In fact, it's happening now and there's a race against time. The point is not to close up situations where someone really wants to bring out their assets in such a way that they do not tax the value that has grown on financial assets, for example. from the moment of their purchase to the moment of the transfer of the assets abroad. I would like to stress once again: We have no objection to moving assets abroad. We don't mind. We just want it to be a fair tax, and we don't want it to be a situation where someone's taking a fortune so they can cash it out later without paying that tax. ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-02-sejm-69-1.u222	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I am very pleased to present the justification of the Act on the Solidarity Fund for the Support of Persons with Disabilities, Parliamentary Printing No 2848. The act on the Solidarity Fund for the Support of Persons with Disabilities aims to introduce into the legal order a state special purpose fund without legal personality, which will focus on social, professional or health support for persons with disabilities. As you will remember, a few months ago the Prime Minister announced the creation of such a fund and today I am pleased to present to you a law which sets up such a fund. The need for regulation arises from the need to provide additional social, occupational or health support to disabled persons. We want the rehabilitation of people with disabilities to be comprehensive, to take into account all the support that a disability or a person with disabilities requires. Currently, support covers only selected aspects of professional and social activation, mainly through the State Fund for the Rehabilitation of Persons with Disabilities. In the meantime, people with disabilities often need more support to benefit from equal opportunities from different areas of life. The Fund will finance new tasks and new forms of support for persons with disabilities, as defined in the programmes of the Council of Ministers or the Minister responsible for social security respectively. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: The 3rd act indicates the fund's revenue catalogue. The main source of this revenue will be a mandatory contribution to the fund amounting to 0,15% the basis for the contribution to the Labour Fund and the solidarity tribute, which will be governed by the Personal Income Tax Act. The amount of the contribution to the Fund will be determined in the Budget Act on the same basis as the amount of the contribution to the Labour Fund. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 6 and 7 are talking about how the fund will be financed and what things will be implemented from the fund. Art. 6 states that the fund will primarily finance government and resorting programmes, including the implementation of the own tasks of local government units in support of people with disabilities. The second type of task will be to promote and support the support system for people with disabilities. The third task will be to address innovative solutions to support people with disabilities. Art. 7 talks about how this will be implemented. This will be implemented through government support programmes for people with disabilities. In addition, the Minister responsible for Social Security will be able to implement Resort support programmes for people with disabilities. According to Art. The 10th draft law will be drawn up by the Minister responsible for Social Security for the calendar year by an annual action plan to support people with disabilities, hereinafter referred to as the support plan. The support plan shall specify in particular the type of tasks or programmes to be carried out and the conditions for their implementation. This plan will be published annually by 30 November in the Public Information Bulletin in accordance with the draft law. At the same time, the support plan will be subject to extensive working consultation with the environment of people with disabilities. Art. The 11th bill tells us how tasks will be carried out. We're pointing to two modes here. One is the selection procedure, the other is open competitions. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: Article 11 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 We specify that the Minister responsible for social security may only order social, professional or health support for persons with disabilities to be carried out without the procedure for the selection of applications or open competitions if there is an important social interest or an important public interest. As far as the main tasks pursued by the fund are concerned, as I have already mentioned, they will be implemented through government and resorting programmes. In connection with the creation of the fund, the bill also introduces a number of amendments to separate laws, mainly in the law on enforcement in the administration, in the Act on income tax on individuals, in the Act on corporate income tax, in the Act on occupational and social rehabilitation and employment of persons with disabilities, in the Act on social security systems, in the Tax Code, in the Act on social assistance, in the Act on promotion of employment and labour market institutions, in the Act on the provision of public-funded health care and in the Act on liability for violation of public finances discipline. Art. 20 of the draft law amends the Act of 26 July 1991 on personal income tax, hereinafter referred to as the Act of PIT. The main changes concern the essence of solidarity tribute, i.e. they specify in particular the entity, object and amount regulated in the added Chapter 6a of the Act on personal income tax. According to Chapter 6a of the PIT Act, the solidarity tribute will be 4%. the basis for its calculation and the deadline for its payment will be 30 April of the calendar year. The calculation of solidarity will be based on a surplus of more than PLN 1 million of the sum of taxable income under the rules laid down in Article. Article 27 (2) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1, 9 and 9a, Art. 30b, Art. 30c and Article 30c 30f PIT Act. In addition to these amendments to the various laws that allow the creation of the fund, Article. 32 We also propose that the Act should enter into force as announced by the Prime Minister on 1 January 2019. Ladies and gentlemen! I ask for kindness for this law, as in order for it to enter on 1 January 2019 it requires urgent procedure. Thank you very much.

The rights of persons with disabilities in the United Nations, in the area of incapacity. Due to the lack of formats easy to read and understand and commonly used persons e.g. with intellectual disabilities, they have limited access to the exercise of their legal capacity and rights. This also means that applications for incapacity are brought before the courts in their cases. In the Family and Care Code, there is an institution called a curator for a person with a disability who could support a person in decision-making, but according to the case law of the Polish courts this cannot include support for persons with intellectual or mental disabilities and their replacement, i.e. representation. That is why the Ombudsman, in his recommendations for this change, states the need to repeal the provisions of the Article. 13 and 16 Civil Code on the incapacity and introduction of new regulations based on the model of supported decision-making. It also recommends stepping up the supervision of care activities and giving direction to persons who have been incapacitated completely and partially until the proposed changes are made, namely the abolition of institutions of incapacity, and finally recommends training for lawyers, especially prosecutors, in the area of the freedoms and rights of persons with disabilities, in particular the right to exercise their full legal capacity. Therefore, this initiative should be used as an opportunity to start this discussion in the assessment of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club. In the course of this discussion, let us argue about the most appropriate solutions, and it is clear that there is a need to fully involve the Minister of Justice in particular, because without the Minister of Justice it is impossible to implement these solutions. However, let us not reject this proposal because it means putting the change back on track. We don't know for how long. If we all agree, and this is my belief that this change must be made, then let us do so without further delay. Thank you. [[Applause]]

Article 6 (6) (a) 3 lit. a Directive 2006/126 of the European Parliament and of the Council of Europe. We proceed with the examination of item 12 of the agenda: the Report of the Committee on Culture and Media and the Committee on Foreign Affairs on the Government's draft Act on the ratification of the Council of Europe Convention on Film Co-production (amended), drawn up at Rotterdam on 30 January 2017 (Prints No. 2790 and 2820). I ask Mr Robert Kołakowski to present a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-02-sejm-69-1.u314	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Culture and Communications and the Committee on Foreign Affairs, I have the honour to present a report to the committee on the Government's draft Act on the ratification of the Council of Europe Convention on Film Co-production (amended), drawn up at Rotterdam on 30 January 2017. The draft submitted concerns a revision of the provisions of the Convention designed to bring producers from non-European countries into line with the situation of producers from the Council of Europe's Member States. It should be stressed that the European Convention on Film Co-production, drawn up in Strasbourg on 2 October 1992, was an act necessary for the design and implementation of audiovisual productions, mainly film productions. It was adopted and ratified by the Republic of Poland in December 2002 and entered into force on 1 March 2003. The amendment to the Convention does not concern its fundamental principles. However, it has proved necessary due to the dynamic development of modern technologies, the growing differences in production finance systems in European countries and the economic and financial changes that have taken place in the audiovisual sector over the last 25 years. As we can read in the explanatory memorandum, the intention of the changes was also to avoid the emerging divergences in interpretation of the provisions of the Convention in the individual countries applying it. The revised Convention sets out the objective of promoting the development of international co-production. In order to achieve this, it was done, among others. changes to the definition, the minimum and maximum shares of the co-producers necessary to apply for support under the Convention have been defined. All co-producers were guaranteed co-ownership of the model copy and participation in the copyright of the film and were allowed to benefit from the support of producers from non-European countries. The application procedure has also been amended. The text of the revised Convention was adopted by the Committee of Ministers of the Council of Europe on 29 June 2016, which concluded the negotiation process. The revised Convention was opened for signature on 30 January Ub. This Regulation shall enter into force on the twentieth day following that of its publication in the Official Journal of the The Council of Ministers of the Republic of Poland adopted a resolution on granting consent to the signing of the Convention on Film Co-production (as amended) in May ub. On 18 July 2017, the Polish Ambassador signed the Convention on behalf of the Republic of Poland. The adoption of the revised Convention will not reduce the fundamental benefits resulting from it for the Polish audiovisual sector, and the extension of its scope should increase the participation of creators and crews from non-European countries in European productions. The Committee on Culture and Media and the Committee on Foreign Affairs after first reading and considering the draft law at its meeting on 11 September this year. asks the High Sejm to adopt the draft law from print no. 2790 without amendments. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-02-sejm-69-1.u316	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform has the honour to present its position on the Act on the ratification of the Council of Europe Convention on Film Co-production (amended), drawn up at Rotterdam on 30 January 2017. The European Convention on Co-production in Strasbourg in 1992 was adopted and ratified by the Republic of Poland in December 2002 and entered into force on 1 March 2003. The amendment of this convention, which does not concern its fundamental principles, has proved necessary due to the dynamic development of modern technologies, the growing differences in production financing systems in European countries, and the economic and financial changes that have taken place in the audiovisual sector over the last 25 years. The first proposal to amend the European Convention on Film Co-production came in 2012 in the form of a request to modernise the provisions on specific issues covered by the Convention. This initiative was based on a Council of Europe report and an expert opinion. In December 2013, the Committee of Ministers of the Council of Europe decided once again to work on preparing a draft amendment to the Convention by 15 experts selected from the 43 countries. The Convention entered into force on 1 October 2017. The Council of Ministers of the Republic of Poland decided on this subject to grant consent to the signing of the Convention. In July 2017, on behalf of the Republic of Poland, the Convention was signed by Janusz Stańczyk, Ambassador of the Extraordinary and Plenipotentiary, Permanent Representative of the Republic of Poland to the Council of Europe. The adoption of the revised Convention will not have any financial implications for the state budget and local government units in the form of a reduction in revenue, an increase in expenditure or a change in their structure. Binding of the Republic of Poland to the Council of Europe Convention on co-production of films in accordance with art. Article 89 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 point 5 of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland should be ratified with the prior consent expressed in the Act. The Civic Platform Parliamentary Club is therefore in favour of the bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

So this could happen: it was assumed, creating the Polish Press Agency system, that this company is always to remain under the sole control of the State, and we are leading to a situation in which other shareholders of this company will appear. At this point we are changing the system of this company, which was to be a single-member company of the State Treasury, and since then remains a private person.
