We proceed with the examination of item 14 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending certain laws in order to introduce simplifications for entrepreneurs in tax and economic law (print No 2862). I would like to ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Entrepreneurship and Technology of Mariusz Haladyja to present the justification for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-02-sejm-69-1.u337	Mr. Marshal! Mr Fellowships and Mr Fellow Members, Before I begin to present individual solutions, at least a few of the chosen solutions of this package, maybe I will put it first, so to speak, in the work of our ministry in general, that is to say, a brief information only about the nature of the regulatory work of our ministry. Firstly, we are preparing projects which contain framework solutions for the pursuit of economic activities, which shape the general principles of doing business. An example is the amendment of the CCPD. before a year or a package of laws under the so-called. the business constitution. Secondly, we are preparing projects that solve specific groups of problems, systemic problems in a given area of activity. This is, for example. the law on succession management or the law on shortening the period of retention of employee records and their electronicisation. Finally, thirdly - and today's project belongs to this category - we are also preparing package laws, which in turn try to limit the point barriers to doing business, some more serious, others less systemicly important, seemingly even minor. On the other hand, all these solutions have one thing in common, i.e. they respond to the demands of entrepreneurs concerning matters that disturb them, I would even say that sometimes they irritate them, in the daily pursuit of business activities. Some of them would be difficult to change by separate laws, even though due to, I don't know, the economics of the legislative process, which is why we prepare such package laws every once in a while. I'm sure you've already read this project. These are issues from various fields that go beyond the strict and literal competence of the Ministry of Entrepreneurship and Technology. Therefore, this law would not have been created without cooperation with other resorts, but above all without cooperation with entrepreneurs. This package contains nearly 50 such concrete, point-by-point changes, which are intended to reduce legal barriers in the business environment, but mainly SMEs. Why is our resort on this subject area primarily targeted in its work? As we will look at the OECD studies, they show that the cost of the various administrative burdens for these smallest companies is even five times higher, and the European Commission estimates that one employee is up to ten times higher than in larger companies. At the same time, these smallest companies generate every second zloty of Polish GDP. The largest share of GDP is in micro-enterprises, close to 1/3 because 30%. Which is perhaps even more important to society, they make up 70% all jobs in the business sector. Therefore, as I am saying, the SME package is the result of talks with companies that we have on the occasion of various meetings, and a review of a number of rules from different branches of law. Through this type of action we remove, as I said, legal barriers, which often irritate our companies on a daily basis, are illogical, unnecessary, etc. We estimate, as regards the financial impact, that thanks to the package of SMEs over 10 years, businesses will save nearly PLN 4 billion in 10 years. So briefly, only briefly can present selected solutions, I will say that we allow e.g. to include in the cost of obtaining income claims under employment contracts, order contracts, work contracts paid to the spouse of a taxable person established or acting as a partner of a company which is not a legal person, i.e. primarily personal companies. What's interesting is that now the spouse's salary It's not at a tax expense, even though everyone else is. This is above all a change advocated by companies for a long time, by family companies, which is why this request comes out. Another thing, also related to small companies, concerns the possibility of clearing one-off: one-off deduction of tax losses up to PLN 5 million. If the loss exceeds the value of PLN 5 million, then this part is settled in subsequent years on current rules, i.e. up to 50% the amount of that loss. Thanks to this, we have a solution that - you can say - throws away bad luggage from the entrepreneur related to failure of investment plans in a given year in order to relieve it, free it from bad, so to speak, debts, bad balance sheet, bad liabilities in companies, which should increase the survival of companies. Another change that will not only be needed for SMEs is the tax exemption of the amount of compensation paid by the insurer in order to restore the damaged asset. So that's what we call a lift relief. There is a pro-investment solution that encourages companies to recreate assets that have been destroyed. Another such, in turn targeted, solution is the corporate tax exemption so-called. alternative investment companies. These are aggregate investment entities, which is something like a small investment fund. In Polish reality today, this solution is not popular at all, because, as a rule, due to tax issues, companies of this type are located abroad, especially Luxembourg is such a popular place. These alternative investment companies are also a great source of capital primarily for start-ups, i.e. small start-ups, which are highly investment-oriented, but also usually small capitalization. Hence this release for ASI to increase the financial vehicles for small companies. As regards VAT, we shorten the deadline for the creditor to apply the so-called rules. relief for bad debts of the present 150 days to 90 days. So this is another reduction in this threshold so as not to reward counterparties who do not pay their creditors and also have a tax advantage. It is well known that cash VAT is not a solution that can be introduced, due to the directive, but this solution is a little closer to some kind of cashibility in the VAT settlement. There are also a few minor changes in the field of VAT: the question of not submitting various forms of return or return declarations in an accelerated period of time. Leaving taxes, and going to e.g. health and safety at work, it must be said that here, in turn, we have a solution that covers nearly 6 million workers, affects more than one million employers. We propose abolishing the employer's absolute obligation to provide periodic health and safety training to administrative and office workers in the third category of risk. I would say that we have such an overly strict implementation of the European Directive today, and that is why it is adapting to certain realities. This is due to observation and conversations with companies, but also conversations with health and safety professionals. This is an obligation which does not particularly benefit, especially in relation to the costs incurred. Not in those categories, not in those positions where, in fact, working conditions do not differ from the risks faced by all citizens in private life, there are the greatest risks. As far as health and safety are concerned, e.g. we increase the employer's ability to perform the tasks of health and safety, we raise this threshold to 50 employees, which is the threshold of a small company. The employer will then be able to perform the tasks of the health and safety services itself without having to separate the health and safety services or entrust these tasks to an isolated worker at the workplace or outside the workplace. While remaining in this area on issues relating to workers, I might mention a solution which is intended to implement a system of requests for L4 exemptions, in particular those short-term exemptions. I stress that this is about making this system work, not putting in place some completely new control system. The point is simply to make those redundancies that are short-lived and which represent more than 50% all sick leave was verifiable. Today's double vision system is, of course, very obnoxious or lame when it comes to the possibility of catching certain irregularities. Depending on the type of employer from 70 to 100% the remuneration is paid during the first 33 days of the illness by the employer himself. I am no longer talking about the fact that, apart from costs, there is always a disorganization of the work of the workplace. Of course, I would stress that this change is not about banning the benefits of medical dismissal, it is about implementing the verification system for these exemptions, which is already in force today. In the field of local taxes, there is a history of the company's postulation, which operates in several municipalities, to standardize the model forms of declarations, in terms of local taxes, i.e. agricultural and forestry taxes, on real estate above all. Today, each municipality issues such a form on its own. We open the way for them to be harmonised by specifying the models of these forms in the regulation by the Minister of Finance. There is also a very useful change concerning companies whose activities involve the conclusion of many contracts subject to compulsory civil law tax, i.e. PCC. We give the opportunity to make such a monthly summary declaration and make a one-off payment of the tax instead of the entire course of those declarations. When it comes to, for example, about accounting issues, we extend the catalogue of units that can apply simplifications in financial reporting. As part of this work I learned what was very interesting for me, that the obligation to keep financial statements in companies is today unlimited, which is actually until the end of the company's existence. We therefore shorten this period to 5 years. This is as much as the tax obligation also implies. Sometimes there are documents that may be needed in this area, but surely there is no need to hold them longer. There are also, of course, specific savings for the storage of these documents. I'm just gonna talk a little bit more about trade law. Here we also have, especially in the case of companies that are the most popular form of doing business in the case of companies governed by commercial law - if I remember correctly, we have more than 400 thousand. companies in the O.O. - dozens of different kinds of changes. I will mention one which is largely focused on the safety of the rotation and the clarification of the situation. This concerns, for example, the issue of submission, the impact of legal acts which are carried out either by the alleged body of a legal person, i.e. wrongly filled, or by an organ well staffed, but exceeding the mandate. We allow this to be confirmed. Today we have many judgments of the court, even the Supreme Court, which are contradictory. There is no single dominant line in terms of impact. Therefore, we allow the possibility to confirm them. That's the way it is in the article. 103. We are also taking advantage of the achievements of the Committee on Civil Law Codification and the Dead. Professor Zbigniew Radwański, who was in charge of this work at the time. Finally, the project also includes, as regards companies or rules, a clear judgment concerning the rules for the representation of the company in liquidation, the resignation of the sole member of the Management Board, the cancellation of the shareholders' meeting, the liability of the company divided for its obligations after the separation. On a daily basis, this causes a lot of trouble in practice regarding the functioning of this most popular company. As I said at the beginning, this initiative is the next step, I stress that this is the stage, [[♪ The bell ♪]] in the tedious, which I should in principle stress, the process of identifying such specific barriers and their gradual removal step by step. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-02-sejm-69-1.u338	Thank you, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. The first Mr Dariusz Starzycki, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-02-sejm-69-1.u339	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the draft law amending certain laws in order to introduce simplifications for entrepreneurs in tax and economic law, print No. 2862. The bill provides for amendments to a number of laws, the main purpose of which is to simplify and facilitate business activity in Poland. This is another package after the package of 100 changes for companies, after the business constitution. They respond to the demands of entrepreneurs, they are expected and agreed with them. The project therefore envisages introducing regulations aimed at reducing the administrative burden on entrepreneurs and extending the existing powers to a wider category of operators. The project also explains some of the interpretation doubts that have been revealed in relation to the pursuit of business activities and introduces tax exemptions for entrepreneurs, as well as tools that minimise the risks associated with tax burdens and conduct business contacts with counterparties who have proved unreliable. The objectives of the project are implemented through changes in two areas: tax law and economic law. The scope of changes in tax law includes, inter alia, harmonisation of the templates for all local tax forms, i.e. agricultural tax, real estate tax and forestry tax, and changes in income taxes providing for: the elimination of obligations which do not directly affect tax settlements and concern circumstances which can be determined on the basis of other documents held by the taxpayer; the increase of the income threshold for obtaining the status of a small taxpayer from EUR 1.2 million to EUR 2 million; the introduction of the possibility to account for the cost of obtaining income of the taxable person's labour value; the introduction of a one-off settlement of the tax loss up to EUR 5 million, as well as a number of amendments to the Accounting Act, among others. shortening the period of mandatory storage of approved financial statements for permanent storage up to 5 years. On the other hand, changes in the area of economic law include, among other things, changes in the area of economic law.: unambiguous definition of the legal effects of activities carried out by an unauthorized person to represent the company, which increases the safety of business turnover; specifying directly in the provisions the possibility of a non-partner to appoint a partner company to the management board; enabling shareholders to adopt resolutions in writing outside the shareholders' meeting, so-called. circulation mode. Changes in the Labour Code include: increasing the ability of an employer with up to 50 employees to perform health and safety tasks and qualifying for no higher risk than the third; reducing the obligation to provide periodic health and safety training for workers with the lowest accident rates and working environments with least health-related factors. This includes about 5 and 6 million administrative and office workers, as the Minister said here. It is also a specific implementation of the constitution for business, which assumes that the directives should be implemented so as not to impose additional burdens on the entrepreneur. Further: the possibility of notification by the Social Security Office or the employer of temporary checks on incapacity to work; the possibility for the county health inspector to receive mobile gastronomy in another county city than a registered company; changes in a number of laws concerning the abolition of the obligation to use a registered stamp or a company stamp as a requirement of formal documents, which are produced by entrepreneurs, and many other facilities, including about 50, which the Minister also referred to. The proposed rules will have a positive impact on the activities of micro and small and medium-sized enterprises. As a rule, new solutions will apply to all entrepreneurs. It is estimated that for 10 years around 4 billion PLN will be in the pocket of entrepreneurs, but in the great majority will be used by the smallest entrepreneurs. The main objective of the new regulation is to reduce administrative burdens and increase access to equity and thus stimulate investment. Therefore, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the draft law in print No 2862 and requests that it be referred to further work in the Extraordinary Committee on Deregulation. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-02-sejm-69-1.u363	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! I would like to present a draft law in the 2855 print on companies investing in real estate rentals. The proposed law aims to stimulate investment in the activities of rental real estate, as well as to increase the involvement of domestic capital, especially small investors, because this law facilitates small investors to invest in real estate rental in such a way that their money is safe and that return on invested capital is attractive to them. The basic construction provided for in the Act is the company investing in the rental property. We predict that it will be a public limited company listed on the stock exchange, which will have at least PLN 50 million of capital. This company will either be able to invest directly in residential properties and rent them, operate rental activities, or may have subsidiaries, i.e. will be able to pack these properties into some packages, and these subsidiaries will rent these residential properties to those already interested. An important fiscal feature of this proposal is that the rental income of the property will be taxed at a very preferential tax rate of 8,5%. This bet depends on... this bet will be... I mean, this tax will be charged not at the time when the rental income is realised, but only when the dividend is paid to the actual investor, i.e. the company investing in the rental of the property will pay the dividend directly to its shareholder. In a situation where, say, the subsidiary only pays dividends to the public limited liability company in respect of which it is a subsidiary, nothing is happening yet, but the transfer of money to the final investor results in a tax obligation. We have established such a very specific way of determining the tax base, namely that we do not allow the depreciation of the property to be deducted. This is the basic cost of a real estate company. But we did it specifically to offset the effect of this very low stake. As a result, such an effective rate of taxation, which will actually apply to these rental revenues, will be around 17%. It could be hesitant, it could be 13, it could be 17, between 8.5 and 19. But what's important is that there's no tax on paying dividends to investors. So this will be the first time in the Polish tax system when an individual investor investing in a capital company receives a dividend that is not subject to dividend tax. There will be only one burden of paying income tax on property rental income. The transfer of this dividend will only entail the obligation to pay the tax. We predict that companies investing in real estate rentals will be required to pay dividends annually and this will be a factor that will encourage individual investors to teach money, to invest in real estate rental companies. If it were to happen that the company investing in the real estate lease would provide for such a scheme that it would have subsidiaries also active in the rental business, then also dividends which will be paid by those subsidiaries will be exempt and will not be taxed. So in short, taxation will only take place when the investor gets the actual flow of money from the company in which he invested to himself. This is the institution that we provide for individual investors and institutional investors. What we expect is that we will stimulate the residential property market, that companies and companies investing in real estate rentals will revive the real estate rental market in Poland. This is a long-term project. It's not gonna be like we're gonna start these companies, and it's gonna be a recovery. On the other hand, we expect that this recovery in the longer term will have positive consequences on the real estate market. Companies investing in real estate rentals are modelled on companies in Western Europe known as real estate investment trust. Such solutions are found in the Netherlands, Belgium, France, the UK, Germany, Italy and other countries. What lies at the heart of this law is that these companies will invest in real estate rentals for residential purposes. We predict that one of the conditions will be that at least 70% the area of the real estate that will be rented shall be the area for residential use. In short, these are the most important assumptions of this project. I hope that I have aroused your interest, and I will be happy to answer all the questions relating to this project. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-02-sejm-69-1.u364	Thank you. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Jerzy Małecki will be the first to speak on behalf of Parliamentary Club Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-02-sejm-69-1.u365	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present a statement concerning the government draft law on companies investing in real estate rentals, Parliamentary Printing No. 2855. The aim of the proposed legal solutions is to introduce into the national legal order the status of a company investing in real estate rental, which is a public limited company and is equivalent to entities operating in the European Union, REIT companies, in countries such as: The Netherlands, Belgium, France, Germany and the United Kingdom, where the first such companies were established in 1969. According to the developer, the introduction of these provisions will have a positive impact on the attractiveness of taking up and doing business in the rental real estate market. It will also contribute to increasing the share of economic operators in this market and thus to reducing its fragmentation. This is due to the provision of corporate income tax preferences, CIT, and personal income tax, PIT, for companies investing in real estate rentals, their subsidiaries, as well as for investors, shareholders, companies investing in real estate rentals. In the case of a company investing in real estate rentals, the more favourable tax conditions would be to defer the CIT tax obligation on income earned, inter alia. from the rental of residential property and from the sale of such immovable property until their equivalent to the payment of dividends to investors, shareholders and the introduction of a CIT rate of 8.5 per cent on the income of the company investing in the rental of residential property. On the other hand, subsidiaries of a real estate company will be able to benefit from a CIT exemption on income derived from the rental of residential property and from the sale of residential property provided that the dividend is paid within a specified period to a real estate company investing in the lease. The provisions of the proposed law also provide for exemption from income tax on capital investment income in a public limited company. This exemption will apply to investors who are taxpayers of both CIT and PIT. The introduction into the national legal system of a company investing in real estate rental does not mean that only such companies will be able to carry out activities involving residential real estate rentals. It is stressed that the decision whether to operate in the regime of a company investing in real estate rentals or to conduct an economic activity on a general basis will be the responsibility of the entity concerned. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Parliamentary Club Law and Justice welcomes the proposal for a law on companies investing in real estate rentals and is therefore in favour of directing it to continue to process. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-02-sejm-69-1.u381	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I'll answer your questions about this bill. Maybe we can explain what we're predicting, how this bill will work. First of all, the Act gives some tax advantages, which is a lower rate when income comes from rentals. It's gotta be 90%. income from the rental of apartments. There's no way to get this status if you invest in commercial property. We did not want to give such a favourable tax solution to companies that invest in commercial real estate from the very beginning. Now, here's the question of the stakes here. I said the nominal rate is 8,5%. Whereas, given that depreciation costs will not cost income, the effective rate will be higher, it will be around 17%. I said it, and I'm supporting it. It's not like we give 8.5%. And it'll always be 8,5%. This may happen if investment costs and rent revenues are arranged so that this rate will be effectively lower, but it is in very specific circumstances. In general, we predict that this effective rate will be approx. 17%. The main advantage of this law is that the investor who invests in a real estate company is confident that he will receive a dividend and that dividend will be taxed once. This means that the tax will be subject not to dividends, but to operating income from rentals - once, without tax on dividends. The first question raised during the debate was whether or not we were predicting how much capital would be invested and how much housing would be built. I will admit that we do not have such data, because, as far as our market is concerned, we do not have the experience or the possibility of predicting what amounts investors are willing to spend on investing in the rental of a particular product, which is an apartment for rent. It is therefore difficult to calculate how many apartments will be available on the market that can be used. The question about local governments is whether local governments can set up such a company. The real estate company is, as I put it, a stock exchange company. In the Act there is a requirement that a company, i.e. a company investing in a real estate lease, must have its shares admitted to trading on the official listing market. In practice, this means above all listing on the stock exchange. Can the local government buy shares of such a company? Maybe. Can it affect its functioning? Maybe if he has the right voting rights to influence such a company. Can such a company be established e.g. by a few local governments who want to join forces to rent apartments in their own territory? It could happen, yeah. I would also like to clarify that we did not anticipate in this law - and it is actually impossible - that this company investing in real estate rentals should be engaged in development activities. She's not gonna build houses. This company will only finance the acquisition of apartments for rent. It's so much... [[Right.]] ...and only in the sense that we specifically did not want such companies to be subject to development risk, because the development risk means a much larger market margin, but this is a higher risk. This is the risk associated with the fact that you need to build a house well, there are various risks associated with improvements, building defects. [[The developer will create himself.]] Yes, the developer will have to do it, only the developer, although it is a risk, has a different margin and its risk premium is different. We wanted to create an opportunity to invest in apartment rentals that would be safe for investors. And this bill is written at this angle. I do not fully understand the question as to what are the reasons for the change in the move-in decision. In fact, it fits in with the apartment + in the sense that there will be companies that will invest in the rental. The question was whether this lease would be long-term or short-term. [[But the surcharge is 15 years.]] She must have a certain ceiling on her rental income. This does not exclude investment in other premises, but only this rental is taxed preferentially, that is, the stream of money that comes from renting apartments, that is, the one that will be taxed according to these rules. [[And if it becomes commercial after 10 years, will it be sold?]] We're predicting that a real estate company can sell the real estate it's invested in. [[After what?]] There is no time limit here, but the point is that it must maintain a certain ceiling on rental revenues. She can sell her own property. Sales of this property will not be subject to this tax regime 8,5% only will be taxed as a normal profit for the disposal of fixed assets. So it's going to be something that we call capital gain tax in jargon. It will be capital gains, i.e. profit from the disposal of fixed assets. And this profit for the disposal of fixed assets will be taxed at a normal rate of 19 percent. There will be costs I have incurred to acquire the property, after a while I will sell this property. Sales price minus acquisition costs - to tax 19%. I hope I've answered all the questions. I'll also tell you if there's a risk that China will invest in other things than housing. They will not be, because this law is so written that it sets a ceiling for rental revenue and assumes this safe path of payment of the lease dividend to the investor. According to the provisions of this Law and the parameters contained therein, it will be impossible to benefit from the fact that it is investing in commercial real estate. So that the law strongly supports only the rental of residential property. Thank you very much.

I understand that those who are satisfied most often have neither children nor grandchildren nor anyone at school. What happened? First of all, the famous two-change and three-change. Such cases, that we had two shifts in education, have always occurred, but since the introduction of education reform, the problem has increased. Now very often children go to school early, before their parents go to work, and come back later than their parents return, because it turns out that first, seventh graders and eighth graders have up to 10 hours and additionally in two- and three-shift mode. Another question: overloaded programs. If you're trying to shove junior high in primary school, then we have the effect that the programs are overloaded. ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-02-sejm-69-1.u427	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The role of the opposition is to criticize, but not in the champ. The project is intended not only to restore the prestige of vocational training in Poland, but mainly to improve its quality and efficiency - the effectiveness of education in schools and educational institutions that train graduates to perform specific professions. It aims to provide a framework for integrating all key vocational training partners into actions to systematically adapt this education to the needs of the labour market. Vocational training is the area of education which, alongside higher education, has the greatest impact on the preparation of modern staff for the Polish economy. According to this project, local authorities will receive a higher education subsidy for pupils in schools who are trained in professions for which there is a higher demand for the labour market. An important legislative solution is the introduction of a new criterion for the distribution of educational subsidies. It is about the diversity of the amounts fixed for vocational pupils in professional education professions of particular importance for culture and national heritage. Another interesting one, and the necessary change is to enable first-grade students and students of the school to pursue a student's internship on the basis of a contract with their employer. Participation in traineeships will enable the pupil to learn the profession in real working conditions and the employer will be able to know and prepare the potential employee accordingly. The costs of cash benefits that the student will receive during his/her internship will be included in the costs of obtaining income. This is certainly a positive change that motivates employers to train professional pupils. In order for the student to succeed, a well-prepared professional teacher is needed, perfecting himself systematically during compulsory professional traineeships. As a result of these trainings, teachers will improve their qualifications, competences and skills, increase their knowledge of new technologies and the functioning of businesses in the industry. Schools will also be able to adapt their education offer more flexibly to the needs of adult learners by introducing shorter forms of teaching. In accordance with the provisions of the project, schools and vocational training establishments will be able to carry out on a separate account income derived from the service activities carried out in the course of training in a particular profession. Changes in the sector education planned since the 2019-2020 school year are also focused on improving and increasing the efficiency of vocational examinations. They should, on the one hand, verify the quality of the training process and, on the other hand, provide a pass to success in the labour market. This project also strengthens the position of education curators who will be responsible for coordinating the organisation of theoretical education for young workers. In this way, they will be educated as closely as possible to their place of residence. It is also very easy for the municipality to organise free transport for children and young people with disabilities and to provide care during transport to secondary schools and vocational training centres in cases other than those indicated in the Education Law as mandatory. It is thanks to employers that it is possible to learn the best quality practical profession, which can be conducted under real working conditions. Compulsory vocational training has also been introduced for vocational teachers in vocational training companies. I was enthusiastic about the provisions that allow students to gain additional skills for their future job. Schools, through the amendment of the law, will gain additional opportunities for students to gain e.g. the skills of the operator of forklifts or SEPs. The proposed law introduces a number of positive changes which, in my opinion, will contribute to improving the quality of professional education in schools and thus to the number of better qualified graduates. Teachers will have the opportunity to upgrade their knowledge from the lectured subject and raise their skills. We must bet on the reliable knowledge of graduates of vocational schools, who, as rightly noted in the draft law, must undertake vocational examinations which allow for a comprehensive assessment of the state of vocational training in Poland. ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-02-sejm-69-1.u492	I'm so sorry, Minister. Ladies and gentlemen! I'm interested in what the Minister says. If you're not here, I'd like to invite you to talk to me on the clowns. [[You're right, Mr. Marshal.]] Minister for Agriculture and Rural Development Jan Krzysztof Ardanowski: Some of these housewives chose the form of associations, but it seems that there is time to sort it out as well. Besides, the rural housewives and rural women expect this. That is why we are presenting with a bill that will allow us to regulate what is most expected, i.e. to give legal personality to the village hostels' Circles. This law also lays down in particular what activities can be carried out by the rural hostel's circles. These include: socio-educational, educational and cultural activities related to rural development, support for entrepreneurship, various actions to improve quality of life. This activity will be carried out by entities with legal personality. And the dysfunction they have suffered so far - the inability to benefit from support from local authorities, which then had huge problems, if they have allocated any resources, even if they have seen and appreciated the activity of rural hostels' circles - will be removed. It will also be possible to use various forms of support in the form of measures from local action groups, measures granted under European Union programmes or national measures. It is also possible to consider, and this law provides for, support in the form of targeted grants from the state budget. That is why we propose that we introduce a very simple and effective way of recording the circles of rural hostesses and giving them personality through the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture - a government institution, universal, having its offices in every district in Poland. Registration will take place at the request of the hostess's group submitted to the county office. The law is attached to the model statute of the village housewives' circle, which can be accepted by founders or founders, because the law does not discriminate against men and probably with well-functioning wheels also men will be able to demonstrate their activity. Around the village hostess may adopt this model statute, possibly in accordance with the law, to prepare his own statute. We respect democracy and the possibility of developing our own statutory solutions, corresponding to the law. The legal personality will also allow the rural hostess' collectives to carry out economic activities. This is very important, because in various forms of their activity this business activity of the wheels wanted to run, and often they could not do so. I will remind you of the scandalous events that the press described a few years ago when housewives who took up culinary activities, some shows at festivals, at harvests, at various kinds of events important to rural communities, were punished by tax officials for selling their productions. We want to solve all of this with this bill. Ladies and gentlemen, the Act also sets out very simplified accounts, records of activities carried out and turnover by the rural hostel circle. The Minister of Finance placed such a simplified accounting method there and it seems that this is also a very good solution. The Act also specifies the minimum number of people who can start a village housewives - that is 10 people. It sets out the principles of internal democracy. It also allows for the creation of associations of several circles of rural hostels in order to implement joint actions, common interests in several villages. It also specifies, by amending several other laws, that the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture will not only be a registering institution, but also the head of the county agency's office will provide comprehensive assistance to ladies who would like to set up and develop activities within the rural hostel's circle, setting up, even if necessary, a special proxy to assist these organisations. Also, a representative of the government for small and medium-sized enterprises may, if necessary, because of the large amount of intensity of organizing rural hostels' circles, appoint a regional representative who will help housewives organize their activities. It seems that these are very beneficial solutions, and for a few days now, since the Council of Ministers has adopted this project, I have received many thanks to the Ministry of Agriculture and to my private addresses from women who believe that some kind of dysfunction, in order not to use the word 'discrimination', these most active women in the countryside is eliminated by this law. Therefore, the question at first whether the rural housewives' wheels are needed must be answered absolutely affirmatively. They're needed. In addition, women are often the most active part of the villagers here, and they take various kinds of actions that serve local communities. In addition to this rather traditional activity, if you can use this word, connected with folk culture, with handicrafts, with works of folk art, there are new issues. It is about needs similar to expectations or needs of other social groups, i.e. access to culture, recreational activities, sports activities and in all other respects concerning villagers. In my opinion, this law is generally too late, and it should have helped women organize for a long time. We are also trying to correct the period when women acted or in unacceptable forms of subordinateism to other socio-professional farmers' organisations, or acted informally, losing the possibility of broad support from both local government and civil society organisations. The Act also provides for the possibility of supporting wheels from the state budget through special grants for activities both this year and the following years. It also provides, of course, that there are no registration fees, all of which are free of charge. The activities of the rural hostess circle for the implementation of the activities set out in the statutes are also free of income tax. I do not know what other arguments to use to persuade the High House to support the bill. [[That's enough.]] It seems that all this, this argument, which I have made very briefly, is sufficient to strongly support the project and help rural women to organise themselves in the way that the 21st century expects. ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-02-sejm-69-1.u527	I have the honour to present the position of the Kukiz Poselski Club15 on the government draft law on the circles of rural hostesses. It is indicated that the KGW are one of the oldest forms of social organizations that operate today in Poland. In many places the Polish KGW was a pillar of local communities, contributing significantly to the formation of rural public space. This is still often the case, but unfortunately - without taking any credit and hard work - KGW are increasingly symbolic. Over the years, the KGW have been effectively supporting our small homelands by participating in the co-organization of schools or voluntary firefighters. KGW took an active part in the education of rural communities in the field of e.g. health protection and support people in difficult situations in their daily responsibilities, such as childcare and household work. Today, the KGW is facing increasing problems, which include, above all, the lack of support from the authorities and the lack of new regulations for the KGW. This is due to the rapid loss of KGW's position in the new institutional structure. The proposed law aims to formalise the functioning of the KGW through the possibility of entering a given circle in the National Register of Rural Houses kept by ARiMR. Under the draft law, the KGW will be promoted to rank with legal personality, self-government, social organisations of village residents, independent of other socio-occupational organisations of farmers, as well as government administration and local self-government units. The project also provides for the possibility of joining and forming KGW unions. Once registered in the KGW register, it will acquire legal personality. This will solve many problems that the KGW has been facing for years. Formization will allow e.g. the application for financial grants for the activities of the KGW. This is not possible now, and local authorities also do not have the possibility to account for the financial support granted to the KGW. The Kukiz Club15 submitted a similar bill aimed at regulating the situation of the rural hostels' circles and removing the remains of the communist public order from the content of the amended laws. The project was coordinated by Elżbieta Zielińska. Our laws - the laws favorable for Poles - should also be adopted, but it often happens that they go into the freezer. Finally, we are pleased that some of the solutions proposed by Kukiz'15 are being implemented. It doesn't matter to us whether the X or Y draft is adopted. It is important for us that the law serves the citizens of Poland. In this case, the proposed normative arrangements should undoubtedly contribute to strengthening and renewing the position of the KGW in the institutional structure. I recommend Kukiz 15 to adopt the bill.

Mr. Marshal! For the first time 1.5 billion PLN is prepared to pay Polish farmer compensation for losses caused by drought, caused by the drought that was this year. Such an amount has never been intended to help a Polish farmer who has suffered from drought. Healthy Polish food too - the idea is to introduce a protein program so that we can recover our protein, our native protein, so that we do not have to use soybeans from GMOs. And, Mr. Marshal, I stress that the law on the circles of rural hostesses is part of this programme, the plan for the village, in order to rebuild the Polish village, because, as Prime Minister Morawiecki of this speaker said, will not be strong Polish economy if there is no strong Polish agriculture. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-02-sejm-69-1.u535	Dear Marshal, I would like to speak in the Seym of the Republic of Poland about a very important, big event that took place yesterday, 2 October 2018. Well, 20 years ago, the State Higher Vocational School in Krosno, a small but important city, sometimes called the small city of Cracow, was in operation. In the Krosno in Podkarpacie just met people who co-created the State Higher Vocational School. I was at this ceremony and therefore I dare to tell about it in the Seym, while reminding the characters of the first rectors of this 20th anniversary, professors of Strutynski, Gonet, Gruchała, Przebinda. Professor Przebinda, the current rector, gave a very humanistic paper devoted to the 20th anniversary of the State Higher Vocational School in Krosno. In my opinion, he didn't miss out on anyone and anything, he appreciated everyone who co-created this school, and everyone who was at that ceremony yesterday. Krosno, a town once a voivodship, at some point stopped being a voivodship. I had the pleasure of being Krosno Voivodeship, knowing that there was a great administrative reform coming in, resulting in Krosno disappearing as a voivodeship, other voivodships will rise, there will be 16 of them - from the old 49 will just remain 16. And then we thought together with many very important people of the then world of politics, economy, the social world about creating such a school in Krosno. I would like to recall today the names of those who helped me, then the humble Krosno voivode, to create this school. It was sleep. Ms Grażyna Gęsicka, Mr Stanisław Zając, Deputy Marshal of the Sejm, Mr Mirosław Handke, Professor, Minister of National Education, Chancellor Franciszek Tereszkiewicz. Later, the idea was supported by the then president Krosna Roman Zimka. There must have been a lot of people who supported this idea. Yesterday, this anniversary was celebrated by many good people, very beautiful meetings, lectures devoted to the 20th anniversary. It turns out that a small city or a medium-sized city can also work well for the development of academic environments. Yesterday, during this ceremony in Krosno, there was also a representative of the Minister of Science and Higher Education, Mr Gowin, Minister Piotr Müller, who handed over a cheque of 23 102 thousand. zł for investments with a beautiful note that the rector will know what to do with these measures. I believe that this approach of authority, the Ministry of Science and Higher Education, deserves attention, recognition. These funds come from the state budget and are dedicated to a modest, but really great school, which allows young people from surrounding villages, districts of the former Krosnieńskie voivodship, and the present Podkarpackie voivodship, to be educated in very good conditions. This has been underlined: under very good conditions. I would also like to thank the great Polish universities: Jagiellonian University, Górniczo-Hutnicza Academy, Rzeszow University of Technology and Rzeszów University for their considerable help in building, co-building scientific staff of this Krosno University. Mr. Marshal, I'd like to invite you to come and visit this environment and see how a good school works in a small town.
